# Autopilot decisions

Non-obvious choices made without asking, with the reason and the alternative. Newest last.

| Date | Decision | Why | Alternative |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-11-10 | Codex runs unattended via `codex exec --approve-for-me` (workspace-write sandbox with network) in a sibling git worktree `../TasksNearMe-ui`, on port 3100, one ticket at a time | Claude and Codex can work in parallel without editing the same files or fighting over port 3000; the sandbox limits what Codex can touch | `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox` (no sandbox; rejected as unnecessary risk) |
| 2026-11-10 | Claude updates `docs/ui/progress.md`; Codex writes only its handoff file | Avoids merge conflicts between the two worktrees | Codex edits its own progress row (caused conflicts in parallel runs) |
| 2026-11-10 | The autopilot commits locally to `main` after green checks; never pushes | The user asked for fully autonomous building; local commits are safe checkpoints | Ask before each commit (would stall the run) |
| 2026-11-11 | Local database: Docker `postgis/postgis:17-3.5` on port 54329, with a separate `tasksnearme_test` database | Docker Desktop was installed and started without interaction; matches production (Postgres + PostGIS) | PGlite or embedded Postgres (no PostGIS) |
| 2026-11-11 | Text primary keys with type prefixes (`usr_…`, `tsk_…`) instead of UUIDv7 | Readable in logs and fixtures; Better Auth uses text ids too | UUIDv7 columns |
| 2026-11-11 | A market is a center + radius; an area is a named center point (a location's area = nearest center) | Enough for one city; avoids drawing polygons now | Area polygons (blueprint 08); can be added later without changing callers |
| 2026-11-11 | DB integration tests run with `npm run test:db`, separate from `npm run check` | Codex's worktree and machines without Docker can still run `check` | Mixing DB tests into `check` |
| 2026-11-11 | Auth: Better Auth with email one-time codes (6 digits, 10 min, 3 attempts), Drizzle adapter, prefixed ids (`usr_`, `ses_`, `acc_`, `ver_`). Until an email provider key exists, mail goes to `dev_outbox_messages` (shown at `/dev/outbox`) | Passwordless sign-in as in the blueprint; no provider key is available (see `blocked.md`) | Magic links; Auth.js |
| 2026-11-11 | Staff two-step check: outside production, code `123456` is accepted and recorded per session in `staff_session_checks`. Real TOTP enrollment comes with the admin staff screens (UI-020) | Gates `/admin` with its final shape now | No staff check until TOTP exists |
| 2026-11-11 | `/dev/sign-in` signs in as a seeded person through the real code flow, with the code created on the server; sign-ins for the same email run one after another | Manual and e2e testing without email; parallel Playwright projects were replacing each other's codes | Reading codes from the dev outbox (raced under parallel tests) |
| 2026-11-11 | `src/proxy.ts` makes an optimistic session-cookie check on `/app/**` (real 307 to sign-in, `next` kept); the layouts do the real check. `/admin` is not matched and shows not-found to non-staff | Real redirect for signed-out visitors; no hint that `/admin` exists | Layout redirects only (streamed, client-side) |
| 2026-11-11 | Next's dev-only "Could not validate `instant`" warnings on gated layouts are expected and left alone | They appear only when the request is meant to be refused (`notFound()` / `redirect()`) | `export const instant = false` on the gated layouts |
| 2026-11-11 | Public marketing data is cached with `"use cache"` (tagged `market:<slug>`), and the pages prerender at build time, so `next build` needs the database | Fast, indexable pages; one cache tag to refresh after admin changes | Render on every request (slower; no static shell) |
| 2026-11-11 | Unknown market or category slugs show the not-found page with `noindex` (status 200, because the response is already streaming) | ISR with Cache Components serves the App Shell first; a real 404 would need a proxy lookup | `dynamicParams = false` (new markets would need a redeploy) |
| 2026-11-11 | The fee engine is pure functions over integer cents (`modules/payments/fees`), tested against every canonical example. Pricing and become-a-helper pages are built from it plus the published market policy | One source of truth for money on every screen | Hard-coded examples |
| 2026-11-11 | Reward program terms are constants (`modules/rewards/programs`) switched by the `reward.*` flags until the rewards module (Phase 7). Founding spots claimed = helpers given a founding number in the market | Matches blueprint 05; no reward tables yet | Reward tables now |
| 2026-11-11 | Playwright e2e: 4 workers and a 15 s expect timeout | One dev server compiles routes on demand; 10+ workers made sign-in flows time out | Run e2e against a production build (needs a test-only sign-in) |
| 2026-11-11 | Help and legal content are Markdown files in `content/`, parsed by a small subset parser (`modules/content/markdown`) into typed `ContentBlock`s; a test checks every file parses and every internal link resolves | Editable text, no HTML injection, no Markdown dependency | A CMS, or MDX (needs a loader and lets JSX into content) |
| 2026-11-11 | Legal documents ship as `Draft 0.1` with a not-in-effect banner and `noindex`; sign-up records `terms_version = draft-0.1` | No lawyer-reviewed text yet (blocked.md) | Leave the legal pages empty |
| 2026-11-11 | Per-IP rate limits apply only when the client address is known and not local (`X-Forwarded-For`); per-email and per-user limits always apply | Local and e2e requests share one loopback address and tripped each other's limits | A higher IP limit (would still collide in local runs) |
| 2026-11-11 | Better Auth reads the session from headers rebuilt from the current cookie jar (`authRequestHeaders`) | After sign-in in a Server Action, `headers()` still carries the old Cookie header, so `refresh()` rendered the signed-out step | Redirect to a fresh request after every step |
| 2026-11-11 | Sign-in steps are decided on the server (`resolveSignInStep`); the phone step appears only when the destination needs it (posting, offers, booking) | Blueprint 02 §1 ("phone verification when needed"); keeps flow logic out of the UI | Ask every new account for a phone |
| 2026-11-11 | Partial Prefetching is per segment (`prefetch = 'partial'` on the `/app` and `/admin` layouts), not global | Globally it embedded a second full payload in every public page that has a dynamic hole (the account area): production home 323 KB, versus 197 KB per segment | Global flag (instant public navigations, double payload) |
| 2026-11-11 | Public dynamic routes await `params` outside Suspense, with `generateStaticParams`. Unknown slugs return a real 404 (supersedes the soft-404 row above) | The recommended pattern without global Partial Prefetching: each URL gets its own cached render | Await inside Suspense (soft 404s, shared fallback) |
| 2026-11-11 | `PublicShell` receives the viewer as a promise (UI-005); the public layout has no Suspense wrapper | The page body renders once; only the account area waits for the session | Suspense around the whole shell (sent each page twice) |
| 2026-11-11 | When a contract adds an error code, Claude adds placeholder copy for it in `messages/en/ui` in the same commit, and the next ticket asks Codex to review the wording | Codex's tests require copy for every code, so new codes would otherwise break `main` | Hold contract changes until Codex is free (stalls the backend) |
| 2026-11-11 | Exact task addresses are stored as AES-256-GCM JSON in `task_locations.address_enc`; tasks keep only the area and a deterministically fuzzed public point (≤ 300 m from the area center) | Blueprint 06 privacy; the public point never leads to the house | PostGIS points of the real address |
| 2026-11-11 | Prohibited-task checks are whole-word phrase matches against global phrases plus category keywords. Moving keywords are narrowed ("your truck", not "truck") | Loading a rented truck is in scope; transport in the helper's vehicle isn't (blueprint 01 §6.1) | Plain substring matching (blocked ordinary moving tasks) |
| 2026-11-11 | Seeded open tasks get windows relative to the seed date (market time) | The board always has upcoming tasks in dev and e2e | Fixed dates (would expire) |
| 2026-11-11 | `reviews` and `helper_work_samples` tables exist ahead of bookings (`reviews.booking_id` is nullable until the bookings module). Helper rating averages and counts are recomputed from published reviews | Profiles show reviews that match their rating; one source of truth | Show seeded aggregate ratings without reviews ("0 of 112") |
| 2026-11-11 | Photo uploads are re-encoded to WebP (max 2000 px) with `sharp`, which drops all metadata including GPS; originals are never stored. Development keeps files on local disk (`.data/uploads`), served by `/api/files/[id]` after an access check | Blueprint 02 J1 step 4 privacy | Store originals and strip on read |
| 2026-11-11 | Uploads go through a Server Action in development (11 MB body limit). Before launch: presigned direct uploads to R2, wired as a client-side `onUploadPhoto` with the same contract type | Hosted functions cap request bodies (Vercel about 4.5 MB) | Server Action uploads in production |
| 2026-11-11 | "Help me describe this" uses a rule-based drafter in development; production returns `unavailable` until a Claude adapter (official SDK) lands with an API key | No key yet (blocked.md); the UI and contract are final | Ship without the feature |
| 2026-11-11 | Open tasks offer only "Cancel task" for now; "Edit task" and "Invite helpers" appear when they exist (UI-023, UI-010) | The domain decides actions; buttons for missing features would lead nowhere | Show them disabled |
| 2026-11-11 | The app e2e projects depend on an `e2e-setup` project that removes earlier posting-run tasks and reseeds the dev database | Tests that decline offers or cancel tasks can run again; recent-task lists stay predictable | A separate e2e database and server |
| 2026-11-11 | Playwright retries once | The dev server occasionally serves one transient error while compiling (e.g. a half-written manifest); retried tests still show as flaky | No retries (random red runs) |
| 2026-11-11 | Development uses stand-ins for ID verification, payout setup and background checks: `/dev/providers/{identity,payout,background}` pages record an outcome through the same service functions the provider webhooks will call. Production has no provider until Stripe Identity, Connect and Checkr are connected | No accounts or keys yet (never create accounts); the onboarding UI and state rules are final | Skip verification in development |
| 2026-11-11 | A helper is bookable for a category once ID is verified and payouts are active; in-home categories also need a clear background check. The profile goes `active` at that point; `paused` and `suspended` are kept as set | Blueprint 06 §1; helpers can take yard work while the background check runs | Require every check before any booking |
| 2026-11-11 | Paused helpers keep helper mode and the helper navigation | Pausing hides the profile and offers, not the helper's tools | Send paused helpers back to onboarding |
| 2026-11-11 | `POST /dev/cache` (development only) expires the `markets`, `tasks`, `helpers` and market tags. The e2e setup calls it after reseeding | Reseeding changes rows behind the app; a long-running dev server kept serving cached pages (a draft helper's old public profile) | Restart the dev server before every e2e run |
| 2026-11-11 | Booking confirmation and payment (UI-010) go before direct requests. Direct requests, the helper's invite responses and counters, and availability posts move to a new UI-025 | Booking an offer is on the core path (post → offer → book → do → approve → pay); invites are a second way in | One large ticket (delays the core path and the hand-off) |
| 2026-11-11 | The helper feed leaves out the helper's own tasks; a distance limit keeps remote tasks (they have no distance) | Helpers looking for work don't need their own posts; remote work is doable from anywhere | Show own tasks as blocked; drop remote tasks under a limit |
| 2026-11-11 | Distances use the helper's area center and the task's fuzzed public point, shown as bands; the service radius is the onboarding option the stored kilometres came from (8 km → 5 mi) | Never exact; matches what the helper picked | Raw kilometres (4.97 mi blocked tasks at 5 mi) |
| 2026-11-11 | Task details, photos and the full description (contact details masked) go only to helpers bookable in at least one category, or who already offered; others get the public excerpt | ui-contract §5 visibility | Show everything to any signed-in helper |
| 2026-11-11 | Paused helpers can still send offers; suspended helpers can't. Pausing hides the profile from browsing and direct requests | Sending an offer is the helper's own explicit choice | Block offers while paused |
| 2026-11-11 | Offers expire lazily: reads and writes that touch a helper's or task's offers mark due ones `expired` and recount. Jobs take this over with the job runner | Correct results now without a scheduler | Wait for the job runner |
| 2026-11-11 | A seeded task with no offers (`tsk_shelves`, Windsor Park) is reserved for the helper e2e; the e2e setup removes its offers each run | Other e2e tests read the desk's offers in parallel; changing Jordan's desk offer would break them | Change the desk offer and serialize the suites |
| 2026-11-11 | Payments in development go through a stateless stand-in shaped like Stripe PaymentIntents: each payment method reference names a test card, and each test card always gives the same outcome (approved, declined, insufficient funds, bank verification). With `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` set, the app refuses to fake payments; production has no provider until the Stripe adapter lands | No Stripe account or keys (blocked.md); every payment state can be tried by hand and in e2e | Skip payments in development |
| 2026-11-11 | Booking an offer creates a `pending_funding` booking with a 30-minute hold (the market policy timer) and one payment row per attempt; success confirms the booking, accepts the offer, declines the other offers, and assigns the task in one transaction under a task row lock. A partial unique index allows one live booking per task | Blueprint 03 §4 and §6.1; double clicks and two tabs can't book twice | Create the booking only after payment succeeds (no hold, no retry state) |
| 2026-11-11 | Unpaid holds expire lazily (on the confirmation page and on paying), like offers, until the job runner exists | Correct results now without a scheduler | Wait for jobs |
| 2026-11-11 | Until the booking page exists (UI-011), `/app/bookings/[bookingId]` redirects the client to the task page and the helper to the task from the helper's side; anyone else gets 404 | Booking links in results and events stay final | Point results at the task page and change them later |
| 2026-11-11 | The seed removes bookings (and their payments and terms) on the seeded tasks before resetting them to open; Maya gets two saved development cards; a moving task with Jordan's hourly offer (Chris) is reserved for the booking e2e | A reseed always leaves the canonical tasks bookable; e2e books a task no other test reads | A separate e2e database |
| 2026-11-11 | Task photos are served to helpers bookable in at least one category while the task is open, and to the booked helper while the booking lasts; evidence and receipts to both people on their booking. The file route checks the file first (owner, staff, work samples) and then what it belongs to | ui-contract §5; the helper task view (UI-009) linked photos the file route refused | Signed URLs per page view |
| 2026-11-11 | Completion evidence follows the category's `required_evidence` and the terms: photos (none for remote work), hours up to the cap for hourly bookings, receipts only with an allowance, a summary for remote work. Work photos and receipts share one upload (`evidence`); ownership is what's checked | Blueprint 02 J6; one upload handler in the UI | Separate receipt uploads |
| 2026-11-11 | Submissions are approved automatically when the window ends (`auto_approve_hours`, 72 h), lazily on reads and actions until the job runner exists | Correct results without a scheduler | Wait for jobs |
| 2026-11-11 | Reporting a problem moves the booking to `disputed` and opens a minimal `support_cases` row (type `dispute`, high priority); the support module grows from there (UI-017/UI-018) | A case exists for staff from day one | Store the report on the booking |
| 2026-11-11 | The booking's calendar file names the general area only, never the exact address | Calendar entries sync elsewhere and outlive the address window | Include the address for the helper |
| 2026-11-11 | A seeded confirmed booking (Ben's TV with Jordan, `bkg_tv`) backs the read-only booking-page e2e | Both e2e projects can read it without booking first | Book in each test |
| 2026-11-11 | The booking page (UI-011) replaces the redirect placeholder at `/app/bookings/[bookingId]`; booking results keep pointing there | The link was final from the start | — |
| 2026-11-11 | New offer action `message_poster` (the helper messages the client from their offer); placeholder copy "Message the client" for Codex to review in UI-014 | Helpers had no way into the offer thread; the client already had `message_helper` | Reuse `message_helper` for both sides (wrong label for helpers) |
| 2026-11-11 | Messaging: one thread per offer or booking, created when first needed; bodies stored as written and masked for the other person until a booking between them is confirmed (the writer always sees their own words); booking updates are status lines in the booking thread; message photos are visible to the thread only; blocked pairs and closed offers or bookings can't send (booking threads close 30 days after completion, 7 after cancellation) | Blueprint 02 J2 step 6, 06, 08 | Mask when storing (loses the original for support) |
| 2026-11-11 | Unit tests get a 15 s timeout and testing-library waits up to 3 s | Codex's independent check failed on an untouched form test that timed out while e2e ran on the same machine; it passes alone | Serialize all checks (slower hand-offs) |
| 2026-11-11 | A second seeded booking (Ruth's bookcase with Jordan) starts `in_progress`, for the completion e2e; the seed reset also clears completions, support cases and booking threads on seeded tasks | Check-in needs the day of the task, and there is no time travel yet | Time travel first (Phase 1 leftover) |
| 2026-11-11 | Cancellation money follows the booking's policy version: client before the free window → full refund (`poster_early`); client after it, or a client no-show after an unanswered can't-access report → the late terms (refund minus the helper's compensation); helper cancels or doesn't show → full refund, plus a strike when late or a no-show. Refunds go through the payments port (one row per refund, idempotency keys); the helper's compensation is an outbox event until the earnings module (UI-016) | Blueprint 03 §4, 04 §6 | Compute money in the UI preview (never: the preview comes from the server) |
| 2026-11-11 | A late update moves the client's no-show reporting time by the minutes the helper gave | Blueprint 03 §4: no-show only with "no check-in or late update" | Block no-show reports entirely after any late update |
| 2026-11-11 | "Message" appears on bookings in every state except `pending_funding` (closed threads stay readable); the helper's offer gets "Message the client" while it's pending, stale or accepted; the seed empties the seeded offers' threads | One way into each conversation from where people already are | A separate "new message" flow |
| 2026-11-11 | Earnings ledger: on completion (approved or automatic), one row per transfer (earnings, reimbursement) from the final numbers, and the client's unused hours or allowance refunded; a late client cancellation or no-show transfers the compensation. Development transfers succeed at once; payouts group transfers older than two days and arrive the next day. Work waiting for approval or held by a problem report shows as `not_due` / `on_hold` from the submitted work, not as ledger rows | Blueprint 04 §5; the ledger only holds money that moved | Ledger rows for pending work |
| 2026-11-11 | Saving a development card "checks it with the bank": the declining test cards are refused; a card paying an open hold can't be removed | Mirrors a SetupIntent check; no orphaned holds | Save any card |
| 2026-11-11 | Reviews: one per person per booking, stored as `submitted`; both publish together when the second arrives, or 14 days after completion (lazily); the helper's rating is recomputed from published client reviews; low ratings are never hidden. "Review" joins the booking page when its page is wired (`REVIEW_PAGE_READY`); "Save helper" works as a tap now | Blueprint 02 J8, F12 | Show reviews immediately |
| 2026-11-11 | Unique-violation checks go through `isUniqueViolation` (the code sits on the Drizzle error's `cause`) | The direct `error.code` check never matched, so races surfaced as 500s | — |
| 2026-11-11 | Heavy work takes a machine-wide lock (`automation/heavy.lock`): Claude's gate (`scripts/gate.mjs`) and the runner's independent `check:ui` wait for each other; the independent check retries once | Two Codex runs were marked failed only because both ran at once (vitest workers couldn't start) | Serialize by hand; more retries |
| 2026-11-11 | e2e specs share `tests/e2e/support/sign-in.ts` (waits up to 45 s for the home; staff land on the two-step check) | The real home compiles slowly on first load; each spec had its own copy | Raise all timeouts |
| 2026-11-11 | The seed reset clears everything that points at the seeded bookings (reviews, earnings and their payouts, refunds, strikes, support cases, completions, threads) and the seeded people's saved helpers | The bookings come back with the same ids, so leftovers attached themselves to the fresh bookings (a review hid "Leave a review") | Fresh booking ids per seed (breaks stable e2e links) |

| 2026-11-11 | Bookings and payments email can't be turned off (receipts and safety messages); other preferences default on except tips and news | Blueprint: transactional mail is required; marketing is opt-in | Make everything optional |
| 2026-11-11 | Data export and account deletion are stored as `privacy_requests` for support to handle within 30 days; deletion waits while the person has an open booking on either side | No automated deletion before support tools (UI-018) exist; open bookings need both people | Delete immediately |
| 2026-11-11 | The messages badge counts unread messages and booking updates in the viewer's threads (the same rule as the inbox's per-thread counts) | One rule everywhere | Count threads |
| 2026-11-11 | The seed reset clears notifications, preferences and privacy requests of the seeded people and seeds four notifications that match the seeded bookings | The e2e changes them; the notifications page needs content | — |
| 2026-11-11 | An interrupted Codex run whose handoff exists is finished with `codex-run.mjs --finish` (independent check, commit, record) instead of being rerun | Codex's work was done; a rerun would reset the branch and repeat 40 minutes of work | Mark failed and rerun |
| 2026-11-11 | e2e failures while Codex runs its own checks are rerun alone before they count; only failures that reproduce block a commit | A full gate had 8 load-related failures that all passed alone | Count every failure |
| 2026-11-11 | Members see the support cases they opened, and cases where support wrote to them (the other person on a booking). | 2026-11-11 | Case events record whose conversation with support they belong to (`party_user_id`) and a visibility (`participants` or `internal` for notes); reads are tracked per person | Each person talks to support privately; adding this later would have exposed earlier messages | One shared case conversation |
| 2026-11-11 | Support requests become blueprint case types where one fits (payment → `payment_issue`, account → `account`, safety → `safety` at high priority); booking questions and "something else" use a new `request` type | Keeps the blueprint's types for routing; general questions had no type | Map everything to `account` |

| 2026-11-11 | The welcome reward's founding cohort is the first 100 helpers by ID-check time across the platform (one market at launch); the $75 monthly cap is checked when a grant is created (UTC calendar month), and a grant over the cap waits for a later month | Blueprint 05 §1–§2 | Count per market now |

| 2026-11-11 | Gift cards go through a development stand-in shaped like Tremendous: it delivers a link to `/dev/gift-cards/[grantId]` at once; the grant id is the order's idempotency key; a set `TREMENDOUS_API_KEY` stops it | No real money or accounts; mirrors LINK delivery | — |
| 2026-11-11 | An in-process job runner (`src/instrumentation.ts` → `src/modules/jobs/runner.ts`) runs the due-work functions every minute under a Postgres advisory lock: offer and unpaid-hold expiry, can't-access expiry, auto-approval, review publishing, notifications. Pages still call them lazily; payouts and rewards stay lazy per person. `JOBS_DISABLED=1` turns it off | Timers fire without page visits; no new infrastructure (graphile-worker can replace it later) | graphile-worker now |
| 2026-11-11 | Money resolutions are previewed on the server (`onPreviewResolution`) with the market's fee rules: settle at P' refunds the difference between the two client totals, the helper gets P' less the helper fee; refund in full returns what's left; goodwill comes from platform funds without touching the helper's earnings. Refund limits per booking: agents $100, leads and finance $500 (finance above that needs a second approval, later) | Blueprint 04 §6 and 08 staff limits; the UI never computes money | Compute in the UI |
| 2026-11-11 | Money resolutions move the money first, then the case resolves (`resolveCaseWithMoney`): release in full completes the booking as submitted; settle rewrites the final numbers at the new price, completes the booking (`settlement`) and refunds the difference as `dispute_settlement`; refund in full returns what's left and cancels (`refunded_in_dispute`); goodwill refunds from platform funds. Transitions are guarded and refunds keyed per case, so a stale page can't pay twice. Staff refunds from the booking page are goodwill or fraud, with the note in the audit log | Blueprint 04 §6; money must never move twice | Resolve first, then move money |
| 2026-11-11 | Money resolutions are offered only for cases about a paid booking: a dispute over submitted work → release, settle, refund in full; an earlier dispute → refund in full; a request → goodwill. Rework waits for the revisit flow (UI-026) | Each resolution needs the booking state it acts on | Offer all, fail at run time |
| 2026-11-11 | When a contract adds a code the UI handles exhaustively (notification types, attention targets), Claude adds minimal placeholders in the UI files that break (an icon, a switch case, test map entries, copy) so `main` keeps compiling, and the next ticket lists them for Codex to review | Tickets must launch from a green `main`; the precedent for placeholder copy | Hold the contract change until Codex builds it |
| 2026-11-11 | The booking page shows the latest problem report to both people (`BookingView.dispute`): the client can always open the case; the helper once support wrote to them. Money is per role: the client's dispute refunds, the helper's earnings. Staff replies and resolutions write `support.replied` / `support.resolved` outbox events that become `support_replied` / `case_resolved` notifications linking to the case | Each person sees only their side; notifications reach people who aren't on the page | Show the whole case to both |

| 2026-11-13 | Staff two-step sign-in uses an authenticator app: TOTP (RFC 6238; 6 digits, 30 seconds, one step either side), built in (`src/platform/auth/totp.ts`), keys encrypted with the field cipher. New staff enroll with a one-time setup code from another admin (24 hours, hashed, replaces earlier codes), then confirm with the app's first code. Each code works once, even for two requests at the same moment. Admins reset a lost authenticator with a reason, never their own, and a reset asks every session of that person for a code again. `npm run staff:setup-code` covers the very first admin, or an admin locked out with no other admin. Development keeps the code 123456 | Blueprint 08 requires TOTP for staff; a setup code means a stolen staff email can't enroll a stranger's phone; no self-service reset, so a stolen admin session can't swap in the thief's phone | Better Auth's two-factor plugin (it covers every account and expects a password, which email-code sign-in doesn't have); passkeys (better still; later) |

| 2026-11-13 | Privacy requests are handled by the app. A copy of the data is prepared by the job runner as one JSON file: the person's own details as written (their addresses decrypted), other people by display name only with contact details masked as in the app, and no tokens, staff notes or documents. An email says it's ready (`data_export_ready`, topic account); it downloads from `/api/account/data-export` for 7 days and is then deleted. A deletion happens 7 days after it's asked for ("Keep my account" takes it back until then), later if a booking, a support case or money on its way is still open. Then the account is anonymized as "Deleted user": contact details, sign-ins, sessions, preferences, photos and saved marks go, open tasks are canceled and pending offers withdrawn, and a last email goes to the old address. Bookings, payments, messages others received and reviews stay, without the name; exact addresses follow the retention timer | Blueprint 08 §5; a week's grace makes a change of mind free; the records other people and the law need survive | Deleting everything at once (breaks other people's bookings and the ledger); support handling requests by hand (slow, easy to get wrong) |
| 2026-11-13 | Retention timers run hourly. An exact address is deleted 180 days after the last booking there, or after its task closed if it was never booked, never while a task, booking, open support case or waiting rebooking proposal uses it. The row stays without the address, and rebooking from it is refused (the client posts again). A conversation's messages and attachments go 2 years after its last message; completion photos and receipt images 2 years after the booking (the expense lines stay); audit entries after 7 years; expired sessions, sign-in codes and old rate-limit windows are swept. Nothing goes while a support case about it is open. Payments, ledger and tax records keep their 7 years; their sweep is due before 2033 | Blueprint 08 §5 | Deleting the whole location row (tasks point at it); deleting expense lines with their receipts (they're part of the money record) |

Tag times go to `cache_tag_invalidations` (migration 0048) on `updateTags` and are read on `refreshTags`, which Next calls once per request before the first read: rows changed since this process last looked, 5 seconds of overlap for late commits, all of them on a process's first read. `get` and `set` never throw; a database error leaves the handler on its own tags with one log line a minute. The job runner deletes rows a week old (`cache.prune_tags`): production drops entries within an hour, the dev server keeps `default`-profile entries longer `SHARED_CACHE_HANDLER=off` falls back to Next's built-in handler. Memory checked: 6,000 stored entries whose streams each held 1 MB of request memory left the heap at 6.5 MB and the process flat near 200 MB (nothing of the stream or the pending entry is kept); a standalone production server under 1,600 cached-page requests levelled off at 480–500 MB with the handler and the same without it. `next build` (standalone) traces the handler and `pg` into the output, at `../cache-handlers/shared-tags.mjs` from `.next` | Blueprint 07 runs 2 web instances; this is the Postgres plan of 2026-11-13, with no new service or dependency | Redis; a handler that stores entries in Postgres too (slower, and nothing needs it) |

That front checks tags in Next's own manifest, and `getCacheHandlers()` (handlers.js) includes the fronts, so `updateTag` reaches them: one process stays correct. Invalidations from another process would reach a dev front only after its background reconcile, which doesn't matter with one dev server. | 2026-11-14 | Scenario-suite rules (5.9, found by the T-06, T-18, T-19 and T-26 tests). (1) A booking's payment settles once, whoever finishes it: `settle()` locks the payment row, as tips do; a second finisher gets "booked", and a late `payment_failed` never overwrites a success. (2) Payouts only move forward and are dated by Stripe's `created` time. A payout that fails after "paid" puts its earnings back on the next payout, and "Action needed" shows while the latest payout failed (or the account itself needs something). (3) Reward budgets (`REWARD_BUDGETS`, rewards/domain.ts) are enforced: grants are made in one transaction behind advisory locks, the helper's (monthly cap) and then the program's (budget). (4) While a chargeback is open, the booking's unsent earnings wait (the transfers job skips them; the earnings page shows `on_hold`), and they go out when it closes, won or lost; money already sent stays the helper's. (5) A lost chargeback writes one balanced `chargeback_loss` journal to a new ledger (migration 0051): `expense:chargeback_losses` and `expense:processing` (Stripe's fee from the dispute, $15 when missing) against `cash:stripe_platform`, and a deferred trigger refuses unbalanced journals. Only chargebacks use the ledger so far; the rest is B-036. (6) Finance and admins download a chargeback's evidence pack as JSON (`/api/admin/bookings/[id]/evidence-pack`): after the two-step check, only for a disputed booking, audited, without the exact address | Each rule closes a gap the tests proved: double confirmations and false refund requests, a budget that was only displayed, an "Action needed" that never cleared, and chargebacks that held nothing and left no record | Optimistic retries instead of the row lock; a budget counter table; holding earnings already paid out (the helper did the work); a PDF evidence pack (finance attaches it to Stripe's form as it is) |

| 2026-11-18 | Uploaded files no longer ship in builds or die with a release (plan 9.3, the owner's "avatar set broke"). The standalone build traced the build machine's `.data/uploads` (test exports, and locally ID photos and proofs) into the bundle, so every preview release shipped a real `.data/uploads` folder and `deploy.sh`'s link to the lasting `/srv/tasksnearme-preview/uploads` landed inside it: uploads went into the release and vanished at the next deploy, and the owner's photo now answers 404. Fixed three ways: the local storage folder resolves at runtime only (`turbopackIgnore`), `bundle.sh` drops `.data`, and `deploy.sh` replaces any `.data/uploads` folder with the link (installed by running `setup-vps.sh` again). A photo whose file is gone shows the initials (`AvatarPhoto`) | A broken image was what people saw; uploads must outlive releases | Restoring the lost files (gone); checking each avatar's file on the server per render (a storage round trip per face) |

| 2026-11-18 | Subscription plans (plan 9.13; owner: "deep think about subscribe plan (ai search, ....) and implement", then "build all without asking"). **Everything works without a plan**: posting, offers, booking, messages, safety, saved searches and their alerts, and the writing help that's free today stay free, and a plan never buys ranking, visibility, a badge or a trust signal. Two optional plans, each on its own, and one person may hold both. **Plus, for clients**: $4.99 a month or $39.99 a year; half the client service fee back on every booking completed while subscribed, up to $15 a calendar month (refunded to the card at completion, like a credit, never more than the platform earned on it after credits); "describe what you need" AI search, 100 a month. **Pro, for helpers**: $7.99 a month or $59.99 a year; half the helper fee back on every booking completed while subscribed, up to $40 a calendar month (a `fee_rebate` transfer with the earnings); AI search for tasks, 100 a month. Free accounts get 5 AI searches a month to try it. The numbers are market policy (`market_policies.plans`, versioned with the fees; null means `DEFAULT_PLANS`), and a booking's rebate uses the plan rates of the policy version it was confirmed under. The monthly caps are 9c's review: uncapped, a half-fee rebate is 5% of a subscriber's volume, so a Pro helper doing $1,000 a month would get $50 back for $7.99, and the busiest helpers (most of the revenue) would cut the platform's take by about a quarter. Yearly plans keep the same monthly caps, and the page and the receipt line say the cap plainly. Billing is Stripe Billing: Checkout in subscription mode with inline prices from the policy (no products to set up in the dashboard), the customer portal to cancel, change the card and see invoices (no plan switching there: to move between monthly and yearly, cancel, then subscribe again once the paid period ends, since a plan can't be bought twice while it's live), and entitlements only from Stripe's subscription events (`checkout.session.completed`, `customer.subscription.*`) through the webhook inbox, never from the browser's return. A plan counts while its subscription is `active`, `trialing` or `past_due` (Stripe's retries) and its paid period hasn't ended; cancelling keeps it to the end of the period paid for. Without Stripe keys (development, tests) a stand-in subscribes at once. Honest terms on the page: the price and what it adds, when it renews, cancel anytime, no trial that turns into a charge, no countdowns or "spots"; a price change applies to new subscriptions only. Deleting an account cancels its subscriptions at once | Fees are what people pay here, so money back on them is the value that adds up for the people who use the site most (break-even shown honestly: Plus after about $100 of tasks a month, Pro after about $160 of earnings), while AI search costs us per call and needs a cap either way. Rebates after completion reuse the credit refund and the transfer paths and leave every quote, snapshot and receipt computation as it is | Lower fee rates at quote time (every quote, snapshot, change, cancellation and refund would have to carry a per-booking rate); free instant payouts for Pro (Stripe charges them by the platform's Connect pricing, which can't be waived per helper); paid placement or a "Pro" badge in lists (buys trust signals); taking today's free writing help behind the plan; one plan for both sides (clients and helpers value different things); a free trial (an easy charge people forget) |

The first message starts it (no empty conversations); one per pair (`threads.kind = 'direct'`, `linked_id` the two ids in order, `task_id` null, `started_by_user_id`). **Limits, generous on purpose:** new conversations a day by Sparks level (10, 15, 25, 40, 60), at least 40 with Plus or Pro, in any rolling 24 hours (the page says how many are left and when the next frees up); the starter can send 3 messages until the other person answers, then any number; the usual 60 messages an hour. **Who may write:** the recipient's setting on the account page (everyone, the default; people they've talked to about a task; nobody), blocks both ways, and the starter's account must be active (restricted or suspended accounts start none). Contact details stay masked until the two have a paid booking together, as in every other conversation; report and block work as in the others. A helper's direct conversation has "Send a task request" beside it (the existing funded direct invite, UC-2), which is how a task is given to someone directly. Notifications say "{name} sent you a message" (no task to name) | People asked to talk before committing to a task, and to come back to someone they liked without posting again. Masking until a paid booking keeps payments on the platform (the reason blueprint 01 had ruled it out); the per-day allowance and the 3 messages before an answer stop cold spam without getting in the way of real use | Messages only between people who've booked (too narrow for what the owner asked); a daily cap on messages rather than on new conversations (punishes real conversations); hard per-message limits by level (the 60-an-hour rate limit already covers floods); letting the recipient's setting cut off conversations already going on (block does that, and it's explicit) |
| 2026-11-21 | **Sparks, TasksNearMe's own score** (owner: "think about tasksnearme own score as Reddit: karma (but different name of score), so to raise karma, users post and can make raise karma with money purchasing"). Named Sparks (Chispas in Spanish). **Earned:** a visible community post +5 (3 a day count), a reply +2 (10 a day), Helpful from someone else +3, Thanks +2, a completed booking +10 (either side), a review written +2; counted from the rows each time, so a removed post, a Helpful taken back or a hidden review stops counting at once (no ledger to drift). **Bought:** packs of 100 for $0.99, 550 for $4.99 and 1,200 for $9.99 through Stripe Checkout in payment mode (inline prices, no products in the dashboard), counted only when Stripe says the session is paid for its amount (the return reads the session back; the webhook `checkout.session.completed` or `.async_payment_succeeded` with `metadata.kind = sparks` does the same); the stand-in counts at once. **Levels:** Newcomer 0, Neighbor 50, Good neighbor 250, Pillar 1,000, Local legend 5,000; a level's only effect is the direct-conversation allowance above. **Honesty rules, because Sparks can be bought:** Sparks show only on a person's own public profile and their community author card (like karma on a Reddit profile), and there the bought part always shows beside the total ("312 · Good neighbor (100 bought)"); they never change search order, matching or helper cards in lists, and never sit with the verification badges, ratings or reviews, which stay the trust signals (the 2026-11-18 rule that money never buys ranking or a trust signal holds); they buy nothing and can't be spent, moved or cashed out (not a stored-value wallet, blueprint 01). The page says all of this | Reddit-style standing rewards taking part in the community the portal (9.10) built, and gives the direct-message limits a fair way to grow. Showing the bought part keeps a bought score from passing for an earned one: the FTC's rule on fake reviews and indicators of social media influence (16 CFR 465, since 2024-10-21) covers indicators bought to misrepresent influence for a commercial purpose (not legal advice; for counsel with the other launch items) | A hidden bought part (a helper could buy a reputation that looks earned); Sparks in search ranking or on helper cards in lists (pay-to-rank, ruled out 2026-11-18); spending Sparks on fees or boosts (a stored-value currency, out of scope in blueprint 01 and a money-transmission question); karma from ratings received (ties standing to reviews, which rewards must never do: blueprint 05) |

| 2026-11-22 | **Dependabot pull requests are made again on the work branch, never merged on GitHub** (the owner turned on Dependabot alerts and security updates; the first PR, #1, moved typescript-eslint 8.72.2 → 8.73.0 for minimatch's GHSA-g3hv-75wg-m249). For each one: check whether the package ships (the build's `.nft.json` traces, `scripts/preview/bundle.sh`) and whether our code reaches the vulnerable path; make the same change from current HEAD in a worktree (`npm update <pkg> --package-lock-only`, then compare its lockfile entries with the PR's); gate it for what it touches (`npm run check` plus a per-rule ESLint comparison for lint or type tooling; check, test:db and the e2e build for anything that ships); it lands with the owner's "commit" and goes out with the next ship, and Dependabot closes its PR and the alert itself once main has the fix. A patch or minor bump of a development tool that changes only the lockfile is taken this way even when the advisory is unreachable: it is the routine update the 2026-11-21 rule waits for. A semver-major bump or a runtime package (next, react, better-auth, drizzle-orm, stripe, pg) gets its own worktree, the full gate and a word to the owner first. An alert with no fixed version on a path we never run is dismissed on GitHub as "vulnerable code is not actually used", with the owner's OK, and rechecked when upstream releases a fix: today that is esbuild ≤ 0.24.2 under drizzle-kit 0.31.11's `@esbuild-kit` loader (the advisory covers esbuild's `serve`, which nothing runs; drizzle-kit runs only on this machine; drizzle-kit 1.0 is still a release candidate) | A merge on GitHub is a push to main: main moves before any e2e run, CI then ships it, and main holds a merge commit the feat/* branch lacks, so the next ship refuses to fast-forward (ci.yml, "main has commits that … doesn't"). Dependabot also builds its lockfile on an old main (PR #1 was 39 commits behind, before zxing-wasm) | Merging Dependabot's PRs as they come (skips the e2e gate and blocks the next ship); turning on version updates as well (a PR per package every week for upgrades nothing needs) |

| 2026-11-23 | **Page switches, reworked** (owner: "When page switches, animation is bad in some pages(skeleton and other elements). Even some skeletons are appeared in top of action bar. And also switching page animation is not good. Deep analyze and upgrade all switch animations"). Recorded frame by frame on a phone build: during each switch both pages' snapshots were drawn above everything else, so the old page and the new skeleton covered the tab dock (and, scrolled, the top bar) for about 300 ms; the two pages showed half-faded over each other; the content then replaced its skeleton in one frame; and a switch inside a section (Neighbors to a post, a task to its offer, Help to an article) didn't animate at all, since a route group's template stays mounted below its first segment. Now: **the bars stay put** (the top bars, the dock, the search row, the chat button and window and the toasts get names only while a transition runs, outside the theme switch, and are drawn above the pages; a name makes its element a stacking context, which would otherwise trap the search's suggestions); **one page at a time** (the old page steps out in 120 ms, the new one comes in from 70 ms); **from where it lives** (deeper comes in from the right while the old page drifts left, back up the other way, the same level rises 10 px; PageTransition compares the address it arrives at with the last one, by level, and marks `<html data-page-motion>`, read only by pseudo-element rules); **skeleton to content** (while a page's skeleton shows, its marker holds the page's `update` class at `page-reveal`, so the content arriving is the one update that animates: the skeleton steps out and the content fades up 6 px into its place); **the dock** slides away for a full-screen page and rises back with the tabs, and the chat button glides to its new place; **17 section templates** (community, find-work, saved-searches and an item's own, support, payments, a booking's pages and its changes, tasks, a task, an offer, messages, a city, its category and tasks, help, cities) so switches inside a section move like the others. a tap on a bar during a switch is handed to the bar's control under it (the browser leaves named elements out of hit testing, so it reached the page under the dock), and browser Back and Forward stay instant | The bars belong to no page, so nothing a page does should cover them, and a switch should read as one page leaving and one arriving, in the direction the person went | `transitionTypes` on every link (105 files use next/link directly, and Back carries none); keying the page by pathname (it would remount nested pages and lose the ones Next keeps for Back); clipping the page snapshots above the dock (their position isn't known to CSS); animating Back and Forward (Next shows the kept page without a View Transition, and phones animate their own back gesture, which would play twice); permanent names on the bars (stacking contexts) |

One rating per case, changeable for 7 days after it was first given, then it stays (`expired`); at most 20 ratings or changes an hour per person. Photos are named, not attached. 3 copies a day per case for each person; a refused send says to try again later (`unavailable`) and records nothing else. | 2026-11-23 | **Fewer trips to other pages, second batch: the bell, an offer, a receipt, a proposal, Invite helpers and the public task page's "Make an offer" open over the page** (owner: "I hate to redirect to new pages continually … if possible, use dialog"; redirect review rows 12, 1, 2, 13, 14 and 18). Each is a route dialog (`src/app/[locale]/@modal/(.)app/…`) over the page it was opened from.<br><br>**The bell** opens the notifications over the page, a sheet on phones. A notification marks itself read, then the dialog closes and its page opens. In the dialog the actions sit at its end rather than in a sticky bar.<br><br>**A receipt** opens over the payments list. Print prints the receipt alone at full length: globals.css's print rules now undo the dialog's height, scroll and shift, and the page's scroll lock.<br><br>**A rebook proposal** opens over My clients, the dashboard or a notification. Accepting pays the hold in place, with the bank's step, and the dialog can't be closed meanwhile (`setBusy`); then the new booking or regular visits open in its place. Declining or withdrawing closes it with a toast.<br><br>**Invite helpers** on an open task opens the client's saved helpers over the task, instead of the Saved helpers page. They're listed newest first, each with Request, or with a line saying they don't take this kind of task now. Request opens the request dialog over the task with this task chosen (`routes.app.requestHelper(handle, { taskId })`, `?task=`, at the helper's listed price).<br><br>**The public task page's "Make an offer"** opens the task from the helper's side in a wide dialog over the page. It has the offer form with its bot check, the questions and the Neighbors link, and the area's drawn map (a live map would take the dialog's scroll). The helper feed's task titles still open the task's page.<br><br>**Addresses.** A dialog only gets the page's own address (receipt, proposal) when that page never has another dialog over it. The bell, an offer, Make an offer and Invite helpers have addresses of their own (`/app/notifications/recent`, `…/offers/[offerId]/view`, `/app/find-work/[taskId]/offer`, `/app/tasks/[taskId]/invite`), and loaded on their own they redirect to the real page. Two failures in the e2e run forced this. A dialog at the offer's own address reloaded the page under checkout after a declined card, because a dialog over a page with a dialog version of its own doesn't survive a refresh. And at the find-work page's address, signing back in after being signed out mid-offer lost the offer's words (J-N2), which only the router's in-memory copy of the page keeps, since signing in clears saved drafts.<br><br>**How a dialog leaves.** A new control, `RouteDialogControls.follow(href)`, closes the dialog first, then opens the page, or on wide screens a conversation in the chat window. The frame's link handling, `ActionBar` (results and link actions), the notification rows and the proposal actions all go through it, so no dialog stays over the next page, and a page with a dialog version opens over the page underneath instead of inside the dialog. Links that navigate after their own work carry `data-own-navigation`, and the frame leaves them alone. The chat window's link catch skips links inside a route dialog, which closes first. `useWideRouteDialog` and `WideRouteDialog` widen the frame to a reading column (42rem) for the offer and the task.<br><br>**After signing in.** The sign-in and sign-up pages and the in-app phone and ID steps carry a marker (`AuthPageMarker`). A route dialog that opens over a visible one (signing in goes on to where it was asked from, with the router) loads as its own page instead, so an email's link to a receipt or a proposal, through sign-in, lands on the page rather than on a dialog over the sign-in page with nothing to close to. |

Folds to one line, remembered per conversation in localStorage (open on the page, folded in the window by default). **Share times**: in a direct conversation, or the offer conversation of their own open (pending or stale) offer, an active helper with at least two open slots gets Share times in the "+": choose 2 or 3, a `times_shared` card goes into the conversation (soonest first), the other person hears it as a message; 10 shares an hour. Only the helper's own `active` slots that haven't started; the card shows each slot open, taken (held or booked), past, or no longer offered (canceled), on the slots' market clock; the client's open ones link to the request dialog with that slot chosen (`routes.app.requestHelper(handle, { slotId })`). Chips show whenever the domain offers one, even when the composer is closed (blocked): they're booking actions, not messages. |
